42,1042,2042 --gpu 3\\n\\n    # Dry-run (no LIBERO env, validates arg parsing + MetaBrain loading)\\n    python phase1/evaluate_libero_plus.py --dry_run --gpu 3\\n\\nOutput:\\n    JSON results + ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\nALL_PERTURBATION_TYPES = [\\n    \\\"camera_viewpoints\\\",\\n    \\\"light_conditions\\\",\\n    \\\"background_textures\\\",\\n    \\\"sensor_noise\\\",\\n    \\\"objects_layout\\\",\\n    \\\"robot_initial_states\\\",\\n    \\\"nominal\\\",\\n]\\n\\nPERTURBATION_TO_3CLASS = {\\n    \\\"camera_viewpoints\\\": \\\"visual\\\",\\n    \\\"light_conditions\\\": \\\"visual\\\",\\n    \\\"background_textures\\\": \\\"visual\\\",\\n    \\\"sensor_noise\\\": \\\"visual\\\",\\n    \\\"objects_layout\\\": \\\"spatial\\\",\\n    \\\"robot_initial_states\\\": \\\"physical\\\",\\n    \\\"nominal\\\": \\\"nominal\\\",\\n}\\n\\nTASK_SUITES = [\\\"libero_spatial\\\", \\\"libero_object\\\", \\\"libero_goal\\\", \\\"libero_10\\\"]\\n\\nMAX_STEPS = {\\n    \\\"libero_spatial\\\": 220,\\n    \\\"libero_object\\\": 280,\\n    \\\"libero_goal\\\": 300,\\n    \\\"libero_10\\\": 520,\\n    \\\"libero_90\\\": 400,\\n}\\n\\nREPLAN_STEPS = 5\\nNUM_STEPS_WAIT = 10\\nLIBERO_DUMMY_ACTION = [0.0] * 6 + [-1.0]\\nIMAGE_SIZE = 256\\nMODEL_INPUT_SIZE = 224\\n\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Perturbation -> task filtering (LIBERO-Plus task_classification.json)\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\nimport re as _re\\n\\nTASK_CLASSIFICATION_PATH = \\\"/root/LIBERO-plus/libero/libero/benchmark/task_classification.json\\\"\\n\\n# Map snake_case perturbation_type -> JSON category label (Title Case).\\nPERTURBATION_TO_JSON_CATEGORY = {\\n    \\\"camera_viewpoints\\\":      \\\"Camera Viewpoints\\\",\\n    \\\"light_conditions\\\":       \\\"Light Conditions\\\",\\n    \\\"background_textures\\\":    \\\"Background Textures\\\",\\n    \\\"background_changes\\\":     \\\"Background Textures\\\",   # alias\\n    \\\"sensor_noise\\\":           \\\"Sensor Noise\\\",\\n    \\\"objects_layout\\\":         \\\"Objects Layout\\\",\\n    \\\"robot_initial_states\\\":   \\\"Robot Initial States\\\",\\n    \\\"language_instructions\\\":  \\\"Language Instructions\\\",\\n}\\n\\n_PERTURB_TOKENS_RE = _re.compile(\\n    r\\\"_(add|remove|swap|table|camera|light|view|initstate|language|noise|init|level|sample|tb)(_|\\\\d)\\\",\\n    _re.IGNORECASE,\\n)\\n\\n_task_classification_cache = None\\n_nominal_name_cache = {}\\n\\n\\ndef _load_task_classification():\\n    global _task_classification_cache\\n    if _task_classification_cache is None:\\n        with open(TASK_CLASSIFICATION_PATH) as f:\\n            _task_classification_cache = json.load(f)\\n    return _task_classification_cache\\n\\n\\ndef _list_nominal_task_names(task_suite):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Original LIBERO base task names (no perturbation suffix) for a suite.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if task_suite in _nominal_name_cache:\\n        return _nominal_name_cache[task_suite]\\n    from libero.libero import get_libero_path\\n    bddl_dir = Path(get_libero_path(\\\"bddl_files\\\")) / task_suite\\n    names = []\\n    for f in bddl_dir.iterdir():\\n        if f.suffix != \\\".bddl\\\":\\n            continue\\n        stem = f.stem\\n        if \\\" copy\\\" in stem:\\n            continue\\n        if _PERTURB_TOKENS_RE.search(stem):\\n            continue\\n        names.append(stem)\\n    names = sorted(set(names))\\n    _nominal_name_cache[task_suite] = names\\n    return names\\n\\n\\ndef get_tasks_by_perturbation(task_suite, perturbation_type):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Return indices identifying tasks for the given (suite, perturbation_type).\\n\\n    For the 7 perturbation categories in task_classification.json: returns\\n    non-negative indices into the LIBERO-Plus benchmark task list.\\n    For 'nominal': returns synthetic negative indices that map back to base\\n    LIBERO BDDL files via _nominal_index_to_name().\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if perturbation_type == \\\"nominal\\\":\\n        names = _list_nominal_task_names(task_suite)\\n        return [-(i + 1) for i in range(len(names))]\\n\\n    cat = PERTURBATION_TO_JSON_CATEGORY.get(perturbation_type)\\n    if cat is None:\\n        raise ValueError(\\n            f\\\"Unknown perturbation_type {perturbation_type!r}. \\\"\\n            f\\\"Valid: {sorted(PERTURBATION_TO_JSON_CATEGORY)} or 'nominal'.\\\"\\n        )\\n    tc = _load_task_classification()\\n    target_names = {it[\\\"name\\\"] for it in tc.get(task_suite, []) if it[\\\"category\\\"] == cat}\\n    if not target_names:\\n        return []\\n\\n    from libero.libero import benchmark\\n    bm = benchmark.get_benchmark_dict()[task_suite]()\\n    return [i for i, n in enumerate(bm.get_task_names()) if n in target_names]\\n\\n\\ndef _nominal_index_to_name(task_suite, neg_idx):\\n    names = _list_nominal_task_names(task_suite)\\n    pos = -neg_idx - 1\\n    if pos < 0 or pos >= len(names):\\n        raise IndexError(f\\\"Nominal index {neg_idx} out of range for {task_suite}\\\")\\n    return names[pos]\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Observation preprocessing (from libero_integration.py)\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef _quat2axisangle(quat):\\n    q = np.asarray(quat, dtype=np.float64)\\n    norm = np.linalg.norm(q)\\n    if norm < 1e-8:\\n        return np.zeros(3, dtype=np.float32)\\n    q /= norm\\n    sin_half = np.linalg.norm(q[:3])\\n    if sin_half < 1e-8:\\n        return np.zeros(3, dtype=np.float32)\\n    angle = 2.0 * math.atan2(sin_half, q[3])\\n    axis = q[:3] / sin_half\\n    return (axis * angle).astype(np.float32)\\n\\n\\ndef _resize_with_pad(img, size):\\n    from PIL import Image as PILImage\\n    h, w = img.shape[:2]\\n    scale = min(size / h, size / w)\\n    new_h, new_w = int(h * scale), int(w * scale)\\n    resized = np.array(\\n        PILImage.fromarray(img).resize((new_w, new_h), PILImage.BILINEAR)\\n    )\\n    canvas = np.zeros((size, size, 3), dtype=np.uint8)\\n    y0, x0 = (size - new_h) // 2, (size - new_w) // 2\\n    canvas[y0 : y0 + new_h, x0 : x0 + new_w] = resized\\n    return canvas\\n\\n\\ndef obs_to_model_input(obs, task_description):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Convert LIBERO env observation to dict for MetaBrain.infer().\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # 180-degree rotation (vertical + horizontal flip) to match openpi training data format\\n    agentview = np.ascontiguousarray(obs[\\\"agentview_image\\\"][::-1, ::-1])\\n    wrist = np.ascontiguousarray(obs[\\\"robot0_eye_in_hand_image\\\"][::-1, ::-1])\\n\\n    if agentview.shape[:2] != (MODEL_INPUT_SIZE, MODEL_INPUT_SIZE):\\n        agentview = _resize_with_pad(agentview, MODEL_INPUT_SIZE)\\n    if wrist.shape[:2] != (MODEL_INPUT_SIZE, MODEL_INPUT_SIZE):\\n        wrist = _resize_with_pad(wrist, MODEL_INPUT_SIZE)\\n\\n    state = np.concatenate([\\n        obs[\\\"robot0_eef_pos\\\"],\\n        _quat2axisangle(obs[\\\"robot0_eef_quat\\\"]),\\n        obs[\\\"robot0_gripper_qpos\\\"],\\n    ])\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"observation/image\\\": agentview,\\n        \\\"observation/wrist_image\\\": wrist,\\n        \\\"observation/state\\\": state,\\n        \\\"prompt\\\": str(task_description),\\n    }\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"}

_nominal_index_to_name(task_suite, neg_idx):\\n    names = _list_nominal_task_names(task_suite)\\n    pos = -neg_idx - 1\\n    if pos < 0 or pos >= len(names):\\n        raise IndexError(f\\\"Nominal index {neg_idx} out of range for {task_suite}\\\")\\n    return names[pos]\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Observation preprocessing (from libero_integration.py)\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef _quat2axisangle(quat):\\n    q = np.asarray(quat, dtype=np.float64)\\n    norm = np.linalg.norm(q)\\n    if norm < 1e-8:\\n        return np.zeros(3, dtype=np.float32)\\n    q /= norm\\n    sin_half = np.linalg.norm(q[:3])\\n    if sin_half < 1e-8:\\n        return np.zeros(3, dtype=np.float32)\\n    angle = 2.0 * math.atan2(sin_half, q[3])\\n    axis = q[:3] / sin_half\\n    return (axis * angle).astype(np.float32)\\n\\n\\ndef _resize_with_pad(img, size):\\n    from PIL import Image as PILImage\\n    h, w = img.shape[:2]\\n    scale = min(size / h, size / w)\\n    new_h, new_w = int(h * scale), int(w * scale)\\n    resized = np.array(\\n        PILImage.fromarray(img).resize((new_w, new_h), PILImage.BILINEAR)\\n    )\\n    canvas = np.zeros((size, size, 3), dtype=np.uint8)\\n    y0, x0 = (size - new_h) // 2, (size - new_w) // 2\\n    canvas[y0 : y0 + new_h, x0 : x0 + new_w] = resized\\n    return canvas\\n\\n\\ndef obs_to_model_input(obs, task_description):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Convert LIBERO env observation to dict for MetaBrain.infer().\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # 180-degree rotation (vertical + horizontal flip) to match openpi training data format\\n    agentview = np.ascontiguousarray(obs[\\\"agentview_image\\\"][::-1, ::-1])\\n    wrist = np.ascontiguousarray(obs[\\\"robot0_eye_in_hand_image\\\"][::-1, ::-1])\\n\\n    if agentview.shape[:2] != (MODEL_INPUT_SIZE, MODEL_INPUT_SIZE):\\n        agentview = _resize_with_pad(agentview, MODEL_INPUT_SIZE)\\n    if wrist.shape[:2] != (MODEL_INPUT_SIZE, MODEL_INPUT_SIZE):\\n        wrist = _resize_with_pad(wrist, MODEL_INPUT_SIZE)\\n\\n    state = np.concatenate([\\n        obs[\\\"robot0_eef_pos\\\"],\\n        _quat2axisangle(obs[\\\"robot0_eef_quat\\\"]),\\n        obs[\\\"robot0_gripper_qpos\\\"],\\n    ])\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"observation/image\\\": agentview,\\n        \\\"observation/wrist_image\\\": wrist,\\n        \\\"observation/state\\\": state,\\n        \\\"prompt\\\": str(task_description),\\n    }\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I understand the action pipeline:
1. Model outputs normalized actions (32D, horizon=50)
2. Actions are denormalized with `actions * (a_std + 1e-6) + a_mean`
3. Then delta actions: `actions[:, :, :6] = actions[:, :, :6] + raw_state_t[:6]` (adds back state to get absolute position)
4. Slice to first 7 dimensions: `actions_7dof = actions_np[:, :7]`

The issue could be:
1. Image preprocessing (we fixed the flip, but maybe the normalization is wrong - uint8 vs float)


2. State normalization, action denormalization, or the delta action recovery logic

I should write a diagnostic script that runs a single episode step and captures the raw model output, denormalized actions, and their value ranges to pinpoint where things are going wrong. I also need to check how the `_preprocess_real_obs` method handles image conversion.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
I see a potential issue. After the images are passed as uint8, they go through:
1. `_parse_image` ensures uint8 HWC
2. `jax.tree.map` converts to torch tensor on device (uint8 BHWC)
3. `_preprocess_real_obs` permutes to BCHW

But the model (SigLIP vision tower) likely expects float32 in [-1, 1] range, not uint8 [0, 255]. Let me check how the PI0Pytorch model processes images. 

Looking at the dummy observation setup versus what happens during actual inference, there's a mismatch: the dummy images are created as float32 tensors in the [-1, 1] range with BCHW layout, but the real inference pipeline is passing uint8 images in [0, 255] range through the same preprocessing. The model probably expects normalized float inputs, so this could be a significant issue.

I need to trace through the PI0Pytorch forward pass and the Observation class to see if there's any normalization happening internally that converts uint8 to float in the [-1, 1] range. If not, that's likely the core bug—the image flip was problematic, but the real issue might be that uint8 images aren't being properly normalized before reaching the vision encoder.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"    def _preprocess_observation(self, observation, *, train=True):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Helper method to preprocess observation.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        observation = _preprocessing.preprocess_observation_pytorch(observation, train=train)\\n        return (\\n            list(observation.images.values()),\\n            list(observation.image_masks.values()),\\n            observation.tokenized_prompt,\\n            observation.tokenized_prompt_mask,\\n            observation.state,\\n        )\\n\\n    def sample_noise(self, shape, device):\\n        return torch.normal(\\n            mean=0.0,\\n            std=1.0,\\n            size=shape,\\n            dtype=torch.float32,\\n            device=device,\\n        )\\n\\n    def sample_time(self, bsize, device):\\n        time_beta = sample_beta(1.5, 1.0, bsize, device)\\n        time = time_beta * 0.999 + 0.001\\n        return time.to(dtype=torch.float32, device=device)\\n\\n    def embed_prefix(\\n        self, images, img_masks, lang_tokens, lang_masks\\n    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Embed images with SigLIP and language tokens with embedding layer to prepare\\n        for PaliGemma transformer processing.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        embs = []\\n        pad_masks = []\\n        att_masks = []\\n\\n        # Process images\\n        for img, img_mask in zip(images, img_masks, strict=True):\\n\\n            def image_embed_func(img):\\n                return self.paligemma_with_expert.embed_image(img)\\n\\n            img_emb = self._apply_checkpoint(image_embed_func, img)\\n\\n            bsize, num_img_embs = img_emb.shape[:2]\\n\\n            embs.append(img_emb)\\n            pad_masks.append(img_mask[:, None].expand(bsize, num_img_embs))\\n\\n            # Create attention masks so that image tokens attend to each other\\n            att_masks += [0] * num_img_embs\\n\\n        # Process language tokens\\n        def lang_embed_func(lang_tokens):\\n            lang_emb = self.paligemma_with_expert.embed_language_tokens(lang_tokens)\\n            lang_emb_dim = lang_emb.shape[-1]\\n            return lang_emb * math.sqrt(lang_emb_dim)\\n\\n        lang_emb = self._apply_checkpoint(lang_embed_func, lang_tokens)\\n\\n        embs.append(lang_emb)\\n        pad_masks.append(lang_masks)\\n\\n        # full attention between image and language inputs\\n        num_lang_embs = lang_emb.shape[1]\\n        att_masks += [0] * num_lang_embs\\n\\n        embs = torch.cat(embs, dim=1)\\n        pad_masks = torch.cat(pad_masks, dim=1)\\n        att_masks = torch.tensor(att_masks, dtype=torch.bool, device=pad_masks.device)\\n\\n        # Get batch size from the first dimension of the concatenated tensors\\n        bsize = pad_masks.shape[0]\\n        att_masks = att_masks[None, :].expand(bsize, len(att_masks))\\n\\n        return embs, pad_masks, att_masks\\n\\n    def embed_suffix(self, state, noisy_actions, timestep):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Embed state, noisy_actions, timestep to prepare for Expert Gemma processing.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        embs = []\\n        pad_masks = []\\n        att_masks = []\\n\\n        if not self.pi05:\\n            if self.state_proj.weight.dtype == torch.float32:\\n                state = state.to(torch.float32)\\n\\n            # Embed state\\n            def state_proj_func(state):\\n                return self.state_proj(state)\\n\\n            state_emb = self._apply_checkpoint(state_proj_func, state)\\n\\n            embs.append(state_emb[:, None, :])\\n            bsize = state_emb.shape[0]\\n            device = state_emb.device\\n\\n            state_mask = torch.ones(bsize, 1, dtype=torch.bool, device=device)\\n            pad_masks.append(state_mask)\\n\\n            # Set attention masks so that image and language inputs do not attend to state or actions\\n            att_masks += [1]\\n\\n        # Embed timestep using sine-cosine positional encoding with sensitivity in the range [0, 1]\\n        time_emb = create_sinusoidal_pos_embedding(\\n            timestep, self.action_in_proj.out_features, min_period=4e-3, max_period=4.0, device=timestep.device\\n        )\\n        time_emb = time_emb.type(dtype=timestep.dtype)\\n\\n        # Fuse timestep + action\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 4138 字符] ...\\n\\n   suffix_out = suffix_out.to(dtype=torch.float32)\\n\\n        # Apply gradient checkpointing to final action projection if enabled\\n        def action_out_proj_func(suffix_out):\\n            return self.action_out_proj(suffix_out)\\n\\n        v_t = self._apply_checkpoint(action_out_proj_func, suffix_out)\\n\\n        return F.mse_loss(u_t, v_t, reduction=\\\"none\\\")\\n\\n    @torch.no_grad()\\n    def sample_actions(self, device, observation, noise=None, num_steps=10) -> Tensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Do a full inference forward and compute the action (batch_size x num_steps x num_motors)\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        bsize = observation.state.shape[0]\\n        if noise is None:\\n            actions_shape = (bsize, self.config.action_horizon, self.config.action_dim)\\n            noise = self.sample_noise(actions_shape, device)\\n\\n        images, img_masks, lang_tokens, lang_masks, state = self._preprocess_observation(observation, train=False)\\n\\n        prefix_embs, prefix_pad_masks, prefix_att_masks = self.embed_prefix(images, img_masks, lang_tokens, lang_masks)\\n        prefix_att_2d_masks = make_att_2d_masks(prefix_pad_masks, prefix_att_masks)\\n        prefix_position_ids = torch.cumsum(prefix_pad_masks, dim=1) - 1\\n\\n        # Compute image and language key value cache\\n        prefix_att_2d_masks_4d = self._prepare_attention_masks_4d(prefix_att_2d_masks)\\n        self.paligemma_with_expert.paligemma.language_model.config._attn_implementation = \\\"eager\\\"  # noqa: SLF001\\n\\n        _, past_key_values = self.paligemma_with_expert.forward(\\n            attention_mask=prefix_att_2d_masks_4d,\\n            position_ids=prefix_position_ids,\\n            past_key_values=None,\\n            inputs_embeds=[prefix_embs, None],\\n            use_cache=True,\\n        )\\n\\n        dt = -1.0 / num_steps\\n        dt = torch.tensor(dt, dtype=torch.float32, device=device)\\n\\n        x_t = noise\\n        time = torch.tensor(1.0, dtype=torch.float32, device=device)\\n        while time >= -dt / 2:\\n            expanded_time = time.expand(bsize)\\n            v_t = self.denoise_step(\\n                state,\\n                prefix_pad_masks,\\n                past_key_values,\\n                x_t,\\n                expanded_time,\\n            )\\n\\n            # Euler step - use new tensor assignment instead of in-place operation\\n            x_t = x_t + dt * v_t\\n            time += dt\\n        return x_t\\n\\n    def denoise_step(\\n        self,\\n        state,\\n        prefix_pad_masks,\\n        past_key_values,\\n        x_t,\\n        timestep,\\n    ):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Apply one denoising step of the noise `x_t` at a given timestep.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        suffix_embs, suffix_pad_masks, suffix_att_masks, adarms_cond = self.embed_suffix(state, x_t, timestep)\\n\\n        suffix_len = suffix_pad_masks.shape[1]\\n        batch_size = prefix_pad_masks.shape[0]\\n        prefix_len = prefix_pad_masks.shape[1]\\n\\n        prefix_pad_2d_masks = prefix_pad_masks[:, None, :].expand(batch_size, suffix_len, prefix_len)\\n\\n        suffix_att_2d_masks = make_att_2d_masks(suffix_pad_masks, suffix_att_masks)\\n\\n        full_att_2d_masks = torch.cat([prefix_pad_2d_masks, suffix_att_2d_masks], dim=2)\\n\\n        prefix_offsets = torch.sum(prefix_pad_masks, dim=-1)[:, None]\\n        position_ids = prefix_offsets + torch.cumsum(suffix_pad_masks, dim=1) - 1\\n\\n        # Prepare attention masks\\n        full_att_2d_masks_4d = self._prepare_attention_masks_4d(full_att_2d_masks)\\n        self.paligemma_with_expert.gemma_expert.model.config._attn_implementation = \\\"eager\\\"  # noqa: SLF001\\n\\n        outputs_embeds, _ = self.paligemma_with_expert.forward(\\n            attention_mask=full_att_2d_masks_4d,\\n            position_ids=position_ids,\\n            past_key_values=past_key_values,\\n            inputs_embeds=[None, suffix_embs],\\n            use_cache=False,\\n            adarms_cond=[None, adarms_cond],\\n        )\\n\\n        suffix_out = outputs_embeds[1]\\n        suffix_out = suffix_out[:, -self.config.action_horizon :]\\n        suffix_out = suffix_out.to(dtype=torch.float32)\\n        return self.action_out_proj(suffix_out)\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.49}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"from collections.abc import Sequence\\nimport logging\\n\\nimport torch\\n\\nfrom openpi.shared import image_tools\\n\\nlogger = logging.getLogger(\\\"openpi\\\")\\n\\n# Constants moved from model.py\\nIMAGE_KEYS = (\\n    \\\"base_0_rgb\\\",\\n    \\\"left_wrist_0_rgb\\\",\\n    \\\"right_wrist_0_rgb\\\",\\n)\\n\\nIMAGE_RESOLUTION = (224, 224)\\n\\n\\ndef preprocess_observation_pytorch(\\n    observation,\\n    *,\\n    train: bool = False,\\n    image_keys: Sequence[str] = IMAGE_KEYS,\\n    image_resolution: tuple[int, int] = IMAGE_RESOLUTION,\\n):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Torch.compile-compatible version of preprocess_observation_pytorch with simplified type annotations.\\n\\n    This function avoids complex type annotations that can cause torch.compile issues.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if not set(image_keys).issubset(observation.images):\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\"images dict missing keys: expected {image_keys}, got {list(observation.images)}\\\")\\n\\n    batch_shape = observation.state.shape[:-1]\\n\\n    out_images = {}\\n    for key in image_keys:\\n        image = observation.images[key]\\n\\n        # TODO: This is a hack to handle both [B, C, H, W] and [B, H, W, C] formats\\n        # Handle both [B, C, H, W] and [B, H, W, C] formats\\n        is_channels_first = image.shape[1] == 3  # Check if channels are in dimension 1\\n\\n        if is_channels_first:\\n            # Convert [B, C, H, W] to [B, H, W, C] for processing\\n            image = image.permute(0, 2, 3, 1)\\n\\n        if image.shape[1:3] != image_resolution:\\n            logger.info(f\\\"Resizing image {key} from {image.shape[1:3]} to {image_resolution}\\\")\\n            image = image_tools.resize_with_pad_torch(image, *image_resolution)\\n\\n        if train:\\n            # Convert from [-1, 1] to [0, 1] for PyTorch augmentations\\n            image = image / 2.0 + 0.5\\n\\n            # Apply PyTorch-based augmentations\\n            if \\\"wrist\\\" not in key:\\n                # Geometric augmentations for non-wrist cameras\\n                height, width = image.shape[1:3]\\n\\n                # Random crop and resize\\n                crop_height = int(height * 0.95)\\n                crop_width = int(width * 0.95)\\n\\n                # Random crop\\n                max_h = height - crop_height\\n                max_w = width - crop_width\\n                if max_h > 0 and max_w > 0:\\n                    # Use tensor operations instead of .item() for torch.compile compatibility\\n                    start_h = torch.randint(0, max_h + 1, (1,), device=image.device)\\n                    start_w = torch.randint(0, max_w + 1, (1,), device=image.device)\\n                    image = image[:, start_h : start_h + crop_height, start_w : start_w + crop_width, :]\\n\\n                # Resize back to original size\\n                image = torch.nn.functional.interpolate(\\n                    image.permute(0, 3, 1, 2),  # [b, h, w, c] -> [b, c, h, w]\\n                    size=(height, width),\\n                    mode=\\\"bilinear\\\",\\n                    align_corners=False,\\n                ).permute(0, 2, 3, 1)  # [b, c, h, w] -> [b, h, w, c]\\n\\n                # Random rotation (small angles)\\n                # Use tensor operations instead of .item() for torch.compile compatibility\\n                angle = torch.rand(1, device=image.device) * 10 - 5  # Random angle between -5 and 5 degrees\\n                if torch.abs(angle) > 0.1:  # Only rotate if angle is significant\\n                    # Convert to radians\\n                    angle_rad = angle * torch.pi / 180.0\\n\\n                    # Create rotation matrix\\n                    cos_a = torch.cos(angle_rad)\\n                    sin_a = torch.sin(angle_rad)\\n\\n                    # Apply rotation using grid_sample\\n                    grid_x = torch.linspace(-1, 1, width, device=image.device)\\n                    grid_y = torch.linspace(-1, 1, height, device=image.device)\\n\\n                    # Create meshgrid\\n                    grid_y, grid_x = torch.meshgrid(grid_y, grid_x, indexing=\\\"ij\\\")\\n\\n                    # Expand to batch dimension\\n                    grid_x = grid_x.unsqueeze(0).expand(image.shape[0], -1, -1)\\n                    grid_y = grid_y.unsqueeze(0).expand(image.shape[0], -1, -1)\\n\\n                    # Apply rotation transformation\\n                    grid_x_rot = grid_x * cos_a - grid_y * sin_a\\n                    grid_y_rot = grid_x * sin_a + grid_y * cos_a\\n\\n                    # Stack and reshape for grid_sample\\n                    grid = torch.stack([grid_x_rot, grid_y_rot], dim=-1)\\n\\n                    image = torch.nn.functional.grid_sample(\\n                        image.permute(0, 3, 1, 2),  # [b, h, w, c] -> [b, c, h, w]\\n                        grid,\\n                        mode=\\\"bilinear\\\",\\n                        padding_mode=\\\"zeros\\\",\\n                        align_corners=False,\\n                    ).permute(0, 2, 3, 1)  # [b, c, h, w] -> [b, h, w, c]\\n\\n            # Color augmentations for all cameras\\n            # Random brightness\\n            # Use tensor operations instead of .item() for torch.compile compatibility\\n            brightness_factor = 0.7 + torch.rand(1, device=image.device) * 0.6  # Random factor between 0.7 and 1.3\\n            image = image * brightness_factor\\n\\n            # Random contrast\\n            # Use tensor operations instead of .item() for torch.compile compatibility\\n            contrast_factor = 0.6 + torch.rand(1, device=image.device) * 0.8  # Random factor between 0.6 and 1.4\\n            mean = image.mean(dim=[1, 2, 3], keepdim=True)\\n            image = (image - mean) * contrast_factor + mean\\n\\n            # Random saturation (convert to HSV, modify S, convert back)\\n            # For simplicity, we'll just apply a random scaling to the color channels\\n            # Use tensor operations instead of .item() for torch.compile compatibility\\n            saturation_factor = 0.5 + torch.rand(1, device=image.device) * 1.0  # Random factor between 0.5 and 1.5\\n            gray = image.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)\\n            image = gray + (image - gray) * saturation_factor\\n\\n            # Clamp values to [0, 1]\\n            image = torch.clamp(image, 0, 1)\\n\\n            # Back to [-1, 1]\\n            image = image * 2.0 - 1.0\\n\\n        # Convert back to [B, C, H, W] format if it was originally channels-first\\n        if is_channels_first:\\n            image = image.permute(0, 3, 1, 2)  # [B, H, W, C] -> [B, C, H, W]\\n\\n        out_images[key] = image\\n\\n    # obtain mask\\n    out_masks = {}\\n    for key in out_images:\\n        if key not in observation.image_masks:\\n            # do not mask by default\\n            out_masks[key] = torch.ones(batch_shape, dtype=torch.bool, device=observation.state.device)\\n        else:\\n            out_masks[key] = observation.image_masks[key]\\n\\n    # Create a simple object with the required attributes instead of using the complex Observation class\\n    class SimpleProcessedObservation:\\n        def __init__(self, **kwargs):\\n            for key, value in kwargs.items():\\n                setattr(self, key, value)\\n\\n    return SimpleProcessedObservation(\\n        images=out_images,\\n        image_masks=out_masks,\\n        state=observation.state,\\n        tokenized_prompt=observation.tokenized_prompt,\\n        tokenized_prompt_mask=observation.tokenized_prompt_mask,\\n        token_ar_mask=observation.token_ar_mask,\\n        token_loss_mask=observation.token_loss_mask,\\n    )\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"}

Create rotation matrix\\n                    cos_a = torch.cos(angle_rad)\\n                    sin_a = torch.sin(angle_rad)\\n\\n                    # Apply rotation using grid_sample\\n                    grid_x = torch.linspace(-1, 1, width, device=image.device)\\n                    grid_y = torch.linspace(-1, 1, height, device=image.device)\\n\\n                    # Create meshgrid\\n                    grid_y, grid_x = torch.meshgrid(grid_y, grid_x, indexing=\\\"ij\\\")\\n\\n                    # Expand to batch dimension\\n                    grid_x = grid_x.unsqueeze(0).expand(image.shape[0], -1, -1)\\n                    grid_y = grid_y.unsqueeze(0).expand(image.shape[0], -1, -1)\\n\\n                    # Apply rotation transformation\\n                    grid_x_rot = grid_x * cos_a - grid_y * sin_a\\n                    grid_y_rot = grid_x * sin_a + grid_y * cos_a\\n\\n                    # Stack and reshape for grid_sample\\n                    grid = torch.stack([grid_x_rot, grid_y_rot], dim=-1)\\n\\n                    image = torch.nn.functional.grid_sample(\\n                        image.permute(0, 3, 1, 2),  # [b, h, w, c] -> [b, c, h, w]\\n                        grid,\\n                        mode=\\\"bilinear\\\",\\n                        padding_mode=\\\"zeros\\\",\\n                        align_corners=False,\\n                    ).permute(0, 2, 3, 1)  # [b, c, h, w] -> [b, h, w, c]\\n\\n            # Color augmentations for all cameras\\n            # Random brightness\\n            # Use tensor operations instead of .item() for torch.compile compatibility\\n            brightness_factor = 0.7 + torch.rand(1, device=image.device) * 0.6  # Random factor between 0.7 and 1.3\\n            image = image * brightness_factor\\n\\n            # Random contrast\\n            # Use tensor operations instead of .item() for torch.compile compatibility\\n            contrast_factor = 0.6 + torch.rand(1, device=image.device) * 0.8  # Random factor between 0.6 and 1.4\\n            mean = image.mean(dim=[1, 2, 3], keepdim=True)\\n            image = (image - mean) * contrast_factor + mean\\n\\n            # Random saturation (convert to HSV, modify S, convert back)\\n            # For simplicity, we'll just apply a random scaling to the color channels\\n            # Use tensor operations instead of .item() for torch.compile compatibility\\n            saturation_factor = 0.5 + torch.rand(1, device=image.device) * 1.0  # Random factor between 0.5 and 1.5\\n            gray = image.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)\\n            image = gray + (image - gray) * saturation_factor\\n\\n            # Clamp values to [0, 1]\\n            image = torch.clamp(image, 0, 1)\\n\\n            # Back to [-1, 1]\\n            image = image * 2.0 - 1.0\\n\\n        # Convert back to [B, C, H, W] format if it was originally channels-first\\n        if is_channels_first:\\n            image = image.permute(0, 3, 1, 2)  # [B, H, W, C] -> [B, C, H, W]\\n\\n        out_images[key] = image\\n\\n    # obtain mask\\n    out_masks = {}\\n    for key in out_images:\\n        if key not in observation.image_masks:\\n            # do not mask by default\\n            out_masks[key] = torch.ones(batch_shape, dtype=torch.bool, device=observation.state.device)\\n        else:\\n            out_masks[key] = observation.image_masks[key]\\n\\n    # Create a simple object with the required attributes instead of using the complex Observation class\\n    class SimpleProcessedObservation:\\n        def __init__(self, **kwargs):\\n            for key, value in kwargs.items():\\n                setattr(self, key, value)\\n\\n    return SimpleProcessedObservation(\\n        images=out_images,\\n        image_masks=out_masks,\\n        state=observation.state,\\n        tokenized_prompt=observation.tokenized_prompt,\\n        tokenized_prompt_mask=observation.tokenized_prompt_mask,\\n        token_ar_mask=observation.token_ar_mask,\\n        token_loss_mask=observation.token_loss_mask,\\n    )\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"

### Assistant

_source line 92_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the issue clearly. The `preprocess_observation_pytorch` function:

1. Checks if the image is channels-first by checking if `image.shape[1] == 3`
2. If channels-first, converts to [B, H, W, C] for processing
3. At the end, converts back to [B, C, H, W] if it was originally channels-first

But critically, **it does NOT normalize the image from uint8 [0, 255] to float [-1, 1]**. It expects the input to already be in [-1, 1] range (the training augmentation code converts from [-1, 1] to [0, 1] and back).


The problem is that in the `infer()` method, images come in as uint8 [0, 255] in BCHW format, so when they reach `preprocess_observation_pytorch`, the channels-first check passes and converts to BHWC, but the values are still in the [0, 255] range instead of the expected [-1, 1] range that the model was trained on.

Now I need to check if the `embed_image` function handles this normalization, or if the issue is that the values are being passed directly to the vision encoder without proper scaling.
```

</details>

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
So `embed_image` calls `self.paligemma.model.get_image_features(image)`. This is the SigLIP vision tower. Let me check if the SigLIP model internally normalizes uint8 images. But typically SigLIP expects float32 images in a specific range.

The issue is clear: in the `infer()` method, images go through as uint8 tensors (values 0-255). The `preprocess_observation_pytorch` function doesn't normalize them. The `embed_image` function just calls `get_image_features` directly.

SigLIP's vision tower typically expects float images normalized to a specific range (usually [-1, 1] or with mean/std normalization). Passing uint8 values (0-255) would give garbage outputs.
 
Tracing through the pipeline, I can see the uint8 values persist all the way through: they come in as numpy arrays, get converted to torch tensors while staying uint8, get permuted to the right channel format, pass through preprocessing without any normalization, and finally hit SigLIP expecting float values in [-1, 1]. The solution is to normalize the images to float in the `infer()` method right after parsing them. I'm seeing that the image goes through several transformations but stays as uint8 [0, 255] all the way to the embedding function, when it should be normalized to [-1, 1] for the model that was trained on that range. I need to add normalization somewhere in the pipeline to convert the uint8 values properly before they reach the SigLIP encoder. I see the issue now—`_parse_image` is converting everything to uint8, but the SigLIP encoder actually needs float images in the [-1, 1] range. I need to rewrite this function to output the correct format instead of uint8.

Writing the image conversion function... Normalizing the images to float32 in the [-1, 1] range that the model expects.
```

{
  "server": "westd-34142",
  "command": "cd /root/meta_brain_lora_routing && cat > /tmp/fix_image_norm.py << 'EOF'\nimport re\n\nwith open(\"phase1/meta_brain_assembly.py\", \"r\") as f:\n    content = f.read()\n\n# Replace the _parse_image function to normalize to float [-1, 1]\nold = \"\"\"        # Parse images to uint8 HWC\n        def _parse_image(img):\n            img = np.asarray(img)\n            if np.issubdtype(img.dtype, np.floating):\n                img = (255 * np.clip(img, 0, 1)).astype(np.uint8)\n            if img.ndim == 3 and img.shape[0] == 3:\n                img = einops.rearrange(img, \"c h w -> h w c\")\n            return img\n\n        base_image = _parse_image(obs_dict[\"observation/image\"])\n        wrist_image = _parse_image(obs_dict[\"observation/wrist_image\"])\"\"\"\n\nnew = \"\"\"        # Parse images to float32 HWC in [-1, 1] range (SigLIP expected input)\n        def _parse_image(img):\n            img = np.asarray(img)\n            if img.ndim == 3 and img.shape[0] == 3:\n                img = einops.rearrange(img, \"c h w -> h w c\")\n            if np.issubdtype(img.dtype, np.integer):\n                img = img.astype(np.float32) / 127.5 - 1.0\n            elif np.issubdtype(img.dtype, np.floating):\n                if img.max() > 1.0:\n                    img = img.astype(np.float32) / 127.5 - 1.0\n                else:\n                    img = (np.clip(img, 0, 1).astype(np.float32) * 2.0) - 1.0\n            return img\n\n        base_image = _parse_image(obs_dict[\"observation/image\"])\n        wrist_image = _parse_image(obs_dict[\"observation/wrist_image\"])\"\"\"\n\nif old not in content:\n    print(\"ERROR: old pattern not found!\")\nelse:\n    content = content.replace(old, new)\n    with open(\"phase1/meta_brain_assembly.py\", \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(content)\n    print(\"OK: image normalization fix applied\")\nEOF\npython3 /tmp/fix_image_norm.py",
  "account": "root"
}
```

```json
{
  "server": "westd-34142",
  "command": "cat > /root/meta_brain_lora_routing/phase1/debug_action_values.py << 'PYEOF'\n\"\"\"Diagnostic: run 10 steps of nominal eval and print action values at each stage.\"\"\"\nimport sys\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/root/openpi/src\")\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/root/openpi\")\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/root/meta_brain_lora_routing/phase1\")\n\nimport logging\nlogging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format=\"%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(message)s\")\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom meta_brain_assembly import MetaBrain\nfrom evaluate_libero_plus import create_libero_env, obs_to_model_input, LIBERO_DUMMY_ACTION, NUM_STEPS_WAIT\n\ndevice = \"cuda:0\"\nlog = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# Load model\nmb = MetaBrain.from_dummy(\n    device=device,\n    routing_mode=\"none\",\n    pi0_checkpoint=\"/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/pi0_libero/\",\n)\n\n# Create nominal env\nenv, task_desc, init_states, task_idx, suite_key = create_libero_env(\n    \"libero_spatial\", \"nominal\", 42\n)\nlog.info(f\"Task: {task_desc}\")\nlog.info(f\"Task idx: {task_idx}, Suite: {suite_key}\")\n\n# Reset\nimport random\nrandom.seed(42)\ntrial_idx = random.randint(0, len(init_states) - 1)\nobs = env.set_init_state(init_states[trial_idx])\nfor _ in range(NUM_STEPS_WAIT):\n    obs, _, _, _ = env.step(LIBERO_DUMMY_ACTION)\n\n# Run 10 steps, print action diagnostics\nlog.info(\"=\" * 60)\nlog.info(\"DIAGNOSTICS: First 10 inference steps\")\nlog.info(\"=\" * 60)\n\nfor step in range(10):\n    obs_dict = obs_to_model_input(obs, task_desc)\n    \n    # Print observation info\n    if step == 0:\n        img = obs_dict[\"observation/image\"]\n        log.info(f\"Image dtype={img.dtype}, shape={img.shape}, range=[{img.min():.3f}, {img.max():.3f}]\")\n        state = obs_dict[\"observation/state\"]\n        log.info(f\"State: {state}\")\n    \n    result = mb.infer(obs_dict)\n    actions = result[\"actions\"]  # [H, 7]\n    \n    log.info(f\"Step {step}: action[0]={actions[0]}, range=[{actions.min():.4f}, {actions.max():.4f}]\")\n    \n    # Execute first action\n    action = actions[0].tolist()\n    obs, reward, done, info = env.step(action)\n    \n    if done or reward > 0:\n        log.info(f\"SUCCESS at step {step}!\")\n        break\n\nenv.close()\nmb.cleanup()\nlog.info(\"Done.\")\nPYEOF\necho \"Script written\"",
  "account": "root"
}

Good, the fix is there. So images ARE being normalized to [-1, 1] inside `infer()`. The diagnostics show that the image is uint8 when it enters the debug script (as seen in obs_to_model_input), but once it goes through `infer()`, it gets normalized.

Now let me look at the actual action values:
- Step 0: `[-0.23, 0.02, 0.16, 0.30, -0.27, 0.10, -0.59]`


- Step 1: `[-0.43, 0.02, 0.14, 0.15, -1.40, 0.07, -0.64]`
- Step 4: `[-0.18, 0.15, 0.58, 0.17, -0.86, 0.26, -0.33]`

These are 7D actions representing [x, y, z, rx, ry, rz, gripper]. The gripper values are consistently in the -0.3 to -0.6 range, which seems off since they should be pushing toward -1.0 for closed or 1.0 for open. The position and rotation components are varying more reasonably across steps.

Looking at the actual state representation, I see it's an 8D vector with the end-effector position, rotation in axis-angle form, and two gripper joint positions. The action deltas are being added to the state values, so I need to trace through how the x-axis action translates when combined with the current state x-position.

Let me reconsider the code logic—it's recovering the original delta commands by adding the state back to what the model predicted, which should give me the raw action values. I'm realizing the z-value of 0.16 is suspiciously low compared to the initial state of 1.18—that would place the robot far below the table surface. Let me reconsider what action space LIBERO actually expects. The environment likely takes delta actions (small incremental changes) rather than absolute positions, so I need to verify whether the model is outputting deltas or absolute coordinates and what format `env.step()` actually accepts. I'm realizing the issue might be in how openpi's training data was structured—they likely recorded absolute target positions rather than deltas, then used a DeltaActions transform to normalize by subtracting the current state, so the model learns to predict the difference between target and current position. If that's the case, then adding the state back would correctly recover the absolute target position, but our environment expects deltas, not absolutes. The denormalized model output is already a delta (target minus state), so adding the current state back converts it to an absolute position—but the environment expects deltas. The dummy action in the eval script uses all zeros for the first 6 dimensions to mean "don't move," which confirms the environment controller is set to delta mode, so the fix is to skip adding state back for those dimensions.

Now I'm checking the actual action values to see if this makes sense: if I subtract the state z-position from the denormalized action, I get roughly -1.02, which would be a massive delta (moving a meter down). That seems too large, so I need to look at the normalization statistics to understand what's actually happening.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the norm_stats clearly:

**Action stats:**
- mean: [0.067, 0.064, -0.805, -2.972, 0.227, 0.119, -0.035, 0, ...]
- std: [0.329, 0.420, 0.617, 0.351, 0.910, 0.344, 0.999, 0, ...]

**State stats:**
- mean: [-0.047, 0.034, 0.765, 2.972, -0.220, -0.126, 0.027, -0.027, 0, ...]


- std: [0.105, 0.152, 0.379, 0.344, 0.907, 0.325, 0.014, 0.014, 0, ...]

This is a major insight—dimension 3 has action mean of -2.972 and state mean of 2.972, which are perfect negatives of each other. This strongly indicates the actions are already stored as delta actions (target minus current state), not raw actions. When `use_delta=True`, the denormalization process reverses this by adding the deltas back to the current state.

But here's the problem: LIBERO's environment expects delta actions as input, not absolute targets. So if the denormalized output is already the delta, adding the state back converts it to an absolute target—which is wrong for what the environment needs. The fix would be to skip that addition step and use the denormalized output directly as the delta.

Let me double-check this theory by looking at the actual action and state means to see if they're consistently negatives of each other...

Actually, I should trace through OpenPI's DeltaActions transform more carefully. If the stored demonstrations contain absolute target positions and the transform subtracts state from them, that would explain the relationship I'm seeing.

But then there's a contradiction: the dummy action has zeros for the position deltas, which only makes sense if the environment expects deltas (zero delta = stay put). If it expected absolute positions, zeros would send the robot to the origin.

So the environment definitely expects deltas, which means somewhere in the pipeline the model's output needs to be converted from absolute targets back to deltas before being passed to env.step. The pipeline makes sense now—the model predicts normalized deltas, which get denormalized back to actual position/orientation changes, then added to the current state to get absolute targets. Since LIBERO's OSC_POSE action expects delta commands for position and orientation, the env.step() call should receive those deltas directly without adding state back. I'm realizing there might be a fundamental confusion between two different action spaces: the small per-step deltas that LIBERO's env.step expects (typically ±0.05), versus openpi's DeltaActions which represent the full target offset from current state and can be much larger. I need to check what the actual evaluation code does with these actions.

Looking at how openpi processes the 50-step action chunk, it seems to extract the first 5 steps and apply them sequentially to the environment. The key question is whether these predicted actions are already meant to be per-step deltas or if they need additional processing to become valid environment steps.

I need to figure out what LIBERO's environment actually expects—whether it interprets actions as absolute target positions or relative deltas, which depends on how the robosuite OSC_POSE controller is configured in LIBERO's setup.

_source line 190_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I see it! In openpi's original `LiberoInputs`:
1. Images are passed as **uint8** (via `_parse_image` which converts to uint8)
2. State is passed as-is (not normalized)
3. There's NO image normalization to [-1, 1] in LiberoInputs

And `LiberoOutputs` just slices to first 7 actions.

So the question is: where does openpi normalize images and actions? It must be in the model pipeline itself. Let me look at how openpi's inference pipeline handles the normalization:


The uint8 images get normalized to [-1, 1] somewhere in the model's forward pass, while the action denormalization happens through the DeltaActions and Normalize transforms. I need to trace through the actual model code to find where this image normalization occurs.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"import functools\\n\\nimport jax\\nimport jax.numpy as jnp\\nimport torch\\nimport torch.nn.functional as F  # noqa: N812\\n\\nimport openpi.shared.array_typing as at\\n\\n\\n@functools.partial(jax.jit, static_argnums=(1, 2, 3))\\n@at.typecheck\\ndef resize_with_pad(\\n    images: at.UInt8[at.Array, \\\"*b h w c\\\"] | at.Float[at.Array, \\\"*b h w c\\\"],\\n    height: int,\\n    width: int,\\n    method: jax.image.ResizeMethod = jax.image.ResizeMethod.LINEAR,\\n) -> at.UInt8[at.Array, \\\"*b {height} {width} c\\\"] | at.Float[at.Array, \\\"*b {height} {width} c\\\"]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Replicates tf.image.resize_with_pad. Resizes an image to a target height and width without distortion\\n    by padding with black. If the image is float32, it must be in the range [-1, 1].\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    has_batch_dim = images.ndim == 4\\n    if not has_batch_dim:\\n        images = images[None]  # type: ignore\\n    cur_height, cur_width = images.shape[1:3]\\n    ratio = max(cur_width / width, cur_height / height)\\n    resized_height = int(cur_height / ratio)\\n    resized_width = int(cur_width / ratio)\\n    resized_images = jax.image.resize(\\n        images, (images.shape[0], resized_height, resized_width, images.shape[3]), method=method\\n    )\\n    if images.dtype == jnp.uint8:\\n        # round from float back to uint8\\n        resized_images = jnp.round(resized_images).clip(0, 255).astype(jnp.uint8)\\n    elif images.dtype == jnp.float32:\\n        resized_images = resized_images.clip(-1.0, 1.0)\\n    else:\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\"Unsupported image dtype: {images.dtype}\\\")\\n\\n    pad_h0, remainder_h = divmod(height - resized_height, 2)\\n    pad_h1 = pad_h0 + remainder_h\\n    pad_w0, remainder_w = divmod(width - resized_width, 2)\\n    pad_w1 = pad_w0 + remainder_w\\n    padded_images = jnp.pad(\\n        resized_images,\\n        ((0, 0), (pad_h0, pad_h1), (pad_w0, pad_w1), (0, 0)),\\n        constant_values=0 if images.dtype == jnp.uint8 else -1.0,\\n    )\\n\\n    if not has_batch_dim:\\n        padded_images = padded_images[0]\\n    return padded_images\\n\\n\\ndef resize_with_pad_torch(\\n    images: torch.Tensor,\\n    height: int,\\n    width: int,\\n    mode: str = \\\"bilinear\\\",\\n) -> torch.Tensor:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"PyTorch version of resize_with_pad. Resizes an image to a target height and width without distortion\\n    by padding with black. If the image is float32, it must be in the range [-1, 1].\\n\\n    Args:\\n        images: Tensor of shape [*b, h, w, c] or [*b, c, h, w]\\n        height: Target height\\n        width: Target width\\n        mode: Interpolation mode ('bilinear', 'nearest', etc.)\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        Resized and padded tensor with same shape format as input\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Check if input is in channels-last format [*b, h, w, c] or channels-first [*b, c, h, w]\\n    if images.shape[-1] <= 4:  # Assume channels-last format\\n        channels_last = True\\n        # Convert to channels-first for torch operations\\n        if images.dim() == 3:\\n            images = images.unsqueeze(0)  # Add batch dimension\\n        images = images.permute(0, 3, 1, 2)  # [b, h, w, c] -> [b, c, h, w]\\n    else:\\n        channels_last = False\\n        if images.dim() == 3:\\n            images = images.unsqueeze(0)  # Add batch dimension\\n\\n    batch_size, channels, cur_height, cur_width = images.shape\\n\\n    # Calculate resize ratio\\n    ratio = max(cur_width / width, cur_height / height)\\n    resized_height = int(cur_height / ratio)\\n    resized_width = int(cur_width / ratio)\\n\\n    # Resize\\n    resized_images = F.interpolate(\\n        images, size=(resized_height, resized_width), mode=mode, align_corners=False if mode == \\\"bilinear\\\" else None\\n    )\\n\\n    # Handle dtype-specific clipping\\n    if images.dtype == torch.uint8:\\n        resized_images = torch.round(resized_images).clamp(0, 255).to(torch.uint8)\\n    elif images.dtype == torch.float32:\\n        resized_images = resized_images.clamp(-1.0, 1.0)\\n    else:\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\"Unsupported image dtype: {images.dtype}\\\")\\n\\n    # Calculate padding\\n    pad_h0, remainder_h = divmod(height - resized_height, 2)\\n    pad_h1 = pad_h0 + remainder_h\\n    pad_w0, remainder_w = divmod(width - resized_width, 2)\\n    pad_w1 = pad_w0 + remainder_w\\n\\n    # Pad\\n    constant_value = 0 if images.dtype == torch.uint8 else -1.0\\n    padded_images = F.pad(\\n        resized_images,\\n        (pad_w0, pad_w1, pad_h0, pad_h1),  # left, right, top, bottom\\n        mode=\\\"constant\\\",\\n        value=constant_value,\\n    )\\n\\n    # Convert back to original format if needed\\n    if channels_last:\\n        padded_images = padded_images.permute(0, 2, 3, 1)  # [b, c, h, w] -> [b, h, w, c]\\n        if batch_size == 1 and images.shape[0] == 1:\\n            padded_images = padded_images.squeeze(0)  # Remove batch dimension if it was added\\n\\n    return padded_images\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
FOUND IT! In `/root/openpi/src/openpi/models/model.py` at line 115-120:

```python
# If images are uint8, convert them to [-1, 1] float32.
if data["image"][key].dtype == np.uint8:
    data["image"][key] = data["image"][key].astype(np.float32) / 255.0 * 2.0 - 1.0


```

There's also a torch version of this same normalization in the `Observation.from_dict()` method that handles torch tensors. So the openpi model does handle uint8→float conversion, but I need to verify whether this method is actually being called in our code path through `meta_brain_assembly.py`'s `infer()` function.

Looking at the flow: my fix converts `_parse_image` output to float32 in the [-1, 1] range, then `jax.tree.map` converts it to a torch tensor, `_preprocess_real_obs` permutes the dimensions, and finally `Observation.from_dict` checks the dtype. Since my fix already converted to float32, the uint8 check in `Observation.from_dict` won't trigger, so there's no double normalization happening.

The debug output showing uint8 is from `obs_to_model_input` which runs before `infer()`, so that's expected. The real question is whether `Observation.from_dict` is doing something else with the float32 data that could cause issues.

Let me trace through what happens after my fix: images get converted to float32 in `_parse_image`, then `jax.tree.map` converts them to torch tensors while staying float32, and the permute operation reshapes them from BHWC to BCHW. So `Observation.from_dict` should receive BCHW float32 images, which should be fine.

Then in `preprocess_observation_pytorch`, it detects the channels-first format and converts back to BHWC for processing, then back to BCHW. Since we're in eval mode with no augmentation, the image just passes through unchanged. The action values I'm seeing suggest the pipeline is working correctly.

Now I need to check whether `Observation.from_dict` also handles state and action denormalization, since that's where the real issue might be.

If None, this transform is a no-op.\\n    # See `make_bool_mask` for more details.\\n    mask: Sequence[bool] | None\\n\\n    def __call__(self, data: DataDict) -> DataDict:\\n        if \\\"actions\\\" not in data or self.mask is None:\\n            return data\\n\\n        state, actions = data[\\\"state\\\"], data[\\\"actions\\\"]\\n        mask = np.asarray(self.mask)\\n        dims = mask.shape[-1]\\n        actions[..., :dims] -= np.expand_dims(np.where(mask, state[..., :dims], 0), axis=-2)\\n        data[\\\"actions\\\"] = actions\\n\\n        return data\\n\\n\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass AbsoluteActions(DataTransformFn):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Repacks delta actions into absolute action space.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    # Boolean mask for the action dimensions to be repacked into absolute action space. Length\\n    # can be smaller than the actual number of dimensions. If None, this transform is a no-op.\\n    # See `make_bool_mask` for more details.\\n    mask: Sequence[bool] | None\\n\\n    def __call__(self, data: DataDict) -> DataDict:\\n        if \\\"actions\\\" not in data or self.mask is None:\\n            return data\\n\\n        state, actions = data[\\\"state\\\"], data[\\\"actions\\\"]\\n        mask = np.asarray(self.mask)\\n        dims = mask.shape[-1]\\n        actions[..., :dims] += np.expand_dims(np.where(mask, state[..., :dims], 0), axis=-2)\\n        data[\\\"actions\\\"] = actions\\n\\n        return data\\n\\n\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass TokenizePrompt(DataTransformFn):\\n    tokenizer: _tokenizer.PaligemmaTokenizer\\n    discrete_state_input: bool = False\\n\\n    def __call__(self, data: DataDict) -> DataDict:\\n        if (prompt := data.pop(\\\"prompt\\\", None)) is None:\\n            raise ValueError(\\\"Prompt is required\\\")\\n\\n        if self.discrete_state_input:\\n            if (state := data.get(\\\"state\\\", None)) is None:\\n                raise ValueError(\\\"State is required.\\\")\\n        else:\\n            state = None\\n\\n        if not isinstance(prompt, str):\\n            prompt = prompt.item()\\n\\n        tokens, token_masks = self.tokenizer.tokenize(prompt, state)\\n        return {**data, \\\"tokenized_prompt\\\": tokens, \\\"tokenized_prompt_mask\\\": token_masks}\\n\\n\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass TokenizeFASTInputs(DataTransformFn):\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"

### Assistant

_source line 208_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I understand!

**DeltaActions** (training transform): `actions[:dims] -= state[:dims]`
- Takes absolute actions and converts them to delta-from-state: `delta = action - state`

**AbsoluteActions** (inference transform): `actions[:dims] += state[:dims]`
- Takes delta-from-state and converts back to absolute: `action = delta + state`

The mask controls which dimensions get this treatment. Let me check what mask is used for LIBERO:
```

### Assistant

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
This is CRUCIAL! From the config:

```
# LIBERO already represents actions as deltas, but we have some old Pi0 checkpoints that are trained with this
# extra delta transform.
if self.extra_delta_transform:
    delta_action_mask = _transforms.make_bool_mask(6, -1)
    data_transforms = data_transforms.push(
        inputs=[_transforms.DeltaActions(delta_action_mask)],
        outputs=[_transforms.AbsoluteActions(delta_action_mask)],
    )
```

So there are TWO cases:


1. **No extra_delta_transform**: LIBERO actions are already deltas. The model learns to predict these deltas (after normalization). At inference, just denormalize to get deltas.
2. **With extra_delta_transform** (old checkpoints): Model was trained with DeltaActions applied (delta_action = raw_delta_action - state). At inference, AbsoluteActions is applied (action = model_output + state). The result is the original delta action from the dataset.

The question is: which case is our checkpoint? The norm_stats show `use_delta=True`, and the MetaBrain code adds state back. This suggests the checkpoint was trained WITH the extra delta transform.

I can verify this by checking the action_mean values against the state_mean. The rotation component shows action_mean[3] = -2.972 and state_mean[3] = 2.972, which matches what I'd expect if the model was trained with the delta transform applied—the action statistics would be the difference between delta and state statistics. Following the inference pipeline, the denormalized output gets state added back to recover the original delta action.

But the 0% success rate suggests something's still wrong. Looking at the actual step outputs, I'm decomposing the action values to see what the denormalized deltas should be—subtracting the state from each action dimension to reverse-engineer what the model actually output before the state was added back.

When I work through the math on dimension 2, the model output comes out to around -0.34, which is reasonable. But the final action delta of 0.16 for the z-axis seems unusually large for LIBERO tasks where typical deltas are ±0.05. This makes me wonder if PI0's action chunking—where it plans 50 steps ahead but only executes 5 at a time—means those large deltas are actually target positions for the end of the planning horizon rather than immediate per-step movements.

Looking at how the evaluation code processes actions, each one from the chunk is treated as an individual step delta, not a cumulative target. So I need to verify whether the model is actually predicting per-step deltas or absolute targets across the 50-timestep window. The LIBERO dataset itself stores actions as deltas, so I should check the data conversion script to confirm what format the model is being trained on.

The training pipeline with `extra_delta_transform=True` takes delta actions from the dataset, computes a "super delta" by subtracting the current state, normalizes it, and trains the model on that. At inference, the process reverses: denormalize the model output, add it back to the state to recover the delta action, then pass that to the environment. This should work correctly in theory, but the observed values like z-delta = 0.16 for step 0 seem suspiciously large, so I need to check what typical action magnitudes look like in LIBERO.

Looking at the action statistics, the "super delta" has a mean around 0.067 for the first dimension and standard deviations ranging from 0.329 to 0.999. When I account for the state offset to recover the actual delta distribution, the means become much smaller—around 0.02 to 0.098—which is reasonable and suggests the pipeline is working as intended.

Now I'm checking whether the observed action values align with these distributions. For the z-dimension, I'm computing the combined standard deviation by treating the super delta and state as independent sources of variance, which gives me roughly 0.72. An observed delta of 0.16 would then be about 0.22 standard deviations from the mean, which is well within normal range.

But I realize LIBERO uses the OSC_POSE controller with action clipping limits—typically ±0.05 for position and ±0.25 for rotation per step. So if the network outputs action[2] = 0.16, it gets clipped to +0.05, and action[4] = -0.27 gets clipped to -0.25. The gripper value of -0.59 indicates a close command. I should trace through what the delta mask actually produces to understand the full picture.

````text
`Observation.from_dict` DOES handle uint8→float conversion:```python
for key in data["image"]:
    if data["image"][key].dtype == np.uint8:
        data["image"][key] = data["image"][key].astype(np.float32) / 255.0 * 2.0 - 1.0
    elif hasattr(data["image"][key], "dtype") and data["image"][key].dtype == torch.uint8:
        data["image"][key] = data["image"][key].to(torch.float32) / 255.0 * 2.0 - 1.0
```

So the original code would've caught the uint8 torch tensor from `jax.tree.map` and normalized it properly. But if `Observation.from_dict` was already handling this, then why did the earlier eval runs fail completely? The image normalization can't have been the actual problem.

Let me think through this more carefully. My fix converts to float in the range [-1, 1], but then `Observation.from_dict` wouldn't touch it since it's already float. The formulas are actually identical though — `img / 127.5 - 1.0` is the same as `img / 255.0 * 2.0 - 1.0`. So the image normalization was never the issue causing those failures.

The real problem must be elsewhere. I'm looking at state normalization now — the inference code normalizes the 8D state before padding, but in the openpi pipeline, state normalization is supposed to be handled by the model's transform layer instead. That mismatch could be causing the model to receive incorrectly scaled inputs. transform). If we're manually normalizing and then the model ALSO normalizes... that could be double normalization!

Looking at the PI0 pytorch forward pass, I don't see state normalization happening in the model itself. The state just goes through a linear projection layer from 32D to the model's hidden dimension. The Normalize and Unnormalize transforms are applied in the data pipeline, not during the forward pass.

In the openpi inference pipeline, the flow is: parse raw observations, skip delta actions for state during inference, normalize state and actions using mean/std, tokenize text, pad everything, then run the model. But in our MetaBrain code, we're manually normalizing and then directly calling `sample_actions` without any intermediate transform pipeline. So our manual normalization should be correct as long as it matches what the model expects.

Let me trace through what happens during training to verify: the state goes through the Normalize transform to get `(state - state_mean) / state_std`, and that's what the model learns from. So at inference, we should also provide normalized state. Our code normalizes before padding, which seems right. But I need to check whether the model's `sample_actions` expects normalized actions as output or if it returns raw actions that need unnormalization.

Looking at the openpi pipeline more carefully: the model learns to predict normalized actions through flow matching, then at inference it outputs those normalized actions which get unnormalized back to deltas, then converted to absolute actions by adding the current state. That's exactly what our code does, so the pipeline should be correct.

Now I'm wondering about the `_build_norm_stats` function and whether it's handling the normalization statistics correctly for the "extra delta" case.

_load_norm_stats(checkpoint_dir, device)\\n    if stats.get(\\\"action_mean\\\") is not None:\\n        log.info(\\\"Using JSON norm_stats (state=%dD, action=%dD, use_delta=True)\\\",\\n                 8, 7)\\n        stats[\\\"use_delta\\\"] = True\\n    return stats\\n\\n\\ndef _load_pi0_checkpoint(model: nn.Module, checkpoint_dir: str) -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load PI0 checkpoint, handling both openpi and LeRobot formats.\\n    \\n    Returns dict of normalization buffers extracted from LeRobot checkpoints, or empty dict.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    ckpt_path = os.path.join(checkpoint_dir, \\\"model.safetensors\\\")\\n    state_dict = safetensors.torch.load_file(ckpt_path)\\n    norm_buffers = {}\\n\\n    has_model_prefix = any(k.startswith(\\\"model.\\\") for k in state_dict)\\n    if has_model_prefix:\\n        norm_prefixes = (\\\"normalize_inputs.\\\", \\\"normalize_targets.\\\", \\\"unnormalize_outputs.\\\")\\n        remapped = {}\\n        ignored_count = 0\\n        for k, v if k.startswith(\\\"model.\\\"):\\n                remapped[k[len(\\\"model.\\\"):]] = v\\n            else:\\n                remapped[k] = v\\n        log.info(\\\"LeRobot format detected: stripped %d keys\\\", len(state_dict))\\n\\n    missing, unexpected = model.load_state_dict(state_dict, strict=False)\\n    tied_keys = [k for k in missing if \\\"embed_tokens\\\" in k]\\n    if tied_keys:\\n Merge LoRA: W_eff = W_frozen + (α/r) · (Σ p_k B_k) @ (Σ p_k A_k)\\n      4. Full sample_actions with merged weights → corrected actions\\n      5.
